ever used in that class then there's no

Singleton pattern is extremely useful
when you have some kind of class of
which is only meant to ever exist just
one single instance of that class for
example in games it is very common to
have some kind of General game manager
and again you have just one usually you
also have some kind of audio manager
some kind of input manager and so on
then for single play games you might
just have one player or even just one
world so if you have just one of
something then the single pattern is
actually really great it makes a lot of
sense and makes it really easy to access
that St I instance for example if your
UI code wants to show the player's
Health it doesn't need to have a direct
reference to the player it can just grab
the Singleton reference or if your
player needs some kind of input it
doesn't need a reference to the input
manager it just access the static
instance and that's it so the single ton
is great especially in game development
where you normally have tons of systems
that have a single instance however the
Singleton pattern also has some
potential problems if you Google you
won't find a lot of people arguing that
this is a terrible pattern although I
should say usually those anti-ant
comments are always a little bit too
extreme the Singleton is a tool and just
like any tool it can be used or abused
and in general Singleton seems to be
worse for regular game development not
game development for game development
like I said it does have a ton of
benefits that do outweigh many of the
possible cons and in general if you
write some good clean code you can
actually minimize a lot of those cons
like the main con is simply the fact
that singlet ton are by definition
global data the whole purpose of
Singleton is to have a single instance
of something that you can easily access
from anywhere so because that it means
that it can have many of the negatives
that any kind of global data can have
your code can easily become really messy
with tons and tons of connections
between various classes since Singleton
are so easy to use you might be inclined
to use them all the time and make it so
that every class pretty much depends on
a ton of Singleton classes doing so ends
up with a mess of dependencies ideally
your code in your classes should be as
decoupled as possible you shouldn't have
a ton of connections this advice is
applicable really all the time not just
in relation to single tense but single
tense make violating this guideline very
easy so that is why you need to to be
extra careful and because those issues
of classes becoming coupled with tons
more classes because that testing can
become insanely hard simply because in
order to test something if the class
you're testing accesses some kind of
single ton then that single T needs to
be created in order to test and in turn
maybe that singl ton then uses yet
another singl ton and on and on so you
might end up requiring a ton of setup to
do even just a basic barebones test
another more serious problem is with
regards to multi-thread code you need to
be very careful to make your single
tense thread safe or simply make sure
you only single T from the main thread
for example if you use Unity dots to
write some super performant multi-thread
code the simple way to avoid this
problem is to do all your logic in tons
of threads then at the end after all
those jobs are completed then you do
anything that requires a main thread
like for example accessing The Sound
Manager single T to play some sort of
sound effect so singl ton can indeed do
have some negatives but they're also
extremely powerful and extremely useful
it is a pattern that can be very good
